Let's list the paths from B to M:
1. B → H → G → F → M
2. B → H → G → X → F → M
3. B → H → G → X → K → F → M
4. B → H → G → X → K → J → P → C → O → Y → N → B → (loop back to B)
5. B → N → O → C → P → J → K → X → G → H → B → (loop back to B)

We notice that the paths involve several intermediate nodes. To disjoint these paths, we need to remove critical nodes that are common to multiple paths.

By examining the graph, we see that:
- Removing node H: Disconnects B from H → G → F → M
- Removing node G: Breaks the path through G → X → F → M and also the loop paths through G and X.
- Removing node X: Further ensures that no path goes through G → X → F → M and also prevents any indirect connection through loop paths.

Thus, the minimum number of nodes to remove to ensure that B and M are completely disconnected is 2. The cut set can be either [H, G] or [G, X].

Therefore, the answer is:

Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [H, G] or [G, X]
